The results show that these methods can effectively train smaller and faster NFs with improved sampling quality. While the paper has some limitations, such as hyperparameter sensitivity and instability in certain cases, its potential impact on the practical application of NFs and its contributions to the area make it a strong and important contribution to the research field.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Bridging Video Quality Scoring and Justification via Large Multimodal Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.21011v1)
- **Authors**: Qizhi Xie, Kun Yuan, Yunpeng Qu, Jiachao Gong, Mingda Wu, Ming Sun, Chao Zhou, Jihong Zhu
- **Abstract**: Classical video quality assessment (VQA) methods generate a numerical score to judge a video's perceived visual fidelity and clarity. Yet, a score fails to describe the video's complex quality dimensions, restricting its applicability. Benefiting from the linguistic output, adapting video large multimodal models (LMMs) to VQA via instruction tuning has the potential to address this issue. The core of the approach lies in the video quality-centric instruction data. Previous explorations mainly focus on the image domain, and their data generation processes heavily rely on human quality annotations and proprietary systems, limiting data scalability and effectiveness. To address these challenges, we propose the Score-based Instruction Generation (SIG) pipeline. Specifically, SIG first scores multiple quality dimensions of an unlabeled video and maps scores to text-defined levels. It then explicitly incorporates a hierarchical Chain-of-Thought (CoT) to model the correlation between specific dimensions and overall quality, mimicking the human visual system's reasoning process. The automated pipeline eliminates the reliance on expert-written quality descriptions and proprietary systems, ensuring data scalability and generation efficiency. To this end, the resulting Score2Instruct (S2I) dataset contains over 320K diverse instruction-response pairs, laying the basis for instruction tuning. Moreover, to advance video LMMs' quality scoring and justification abilities simultaneously, we devise a progressive tuning strategy to fully unleash the power of S2I. Built upon SIG, we further curate a benchmark termed S2I-Bench with 400 open-ended questions to better evaluate the quality justification capacity of video LMMs. Experimental results on the S2I-Bench and existing benchmarks indicate that our method consistently improves quality scoring and justification capabilities across multiple video LMMs.
- **Summary**: Okay, here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Bridging Video Quality Scoring and Justification via Large Multimodal Models":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel approach for video quality assessment (VQA) using large multimodal models (LMMs).  Instead of relying solely on numerical scores, which lack detail, the authors propose a framework that enables LMMs to provide natural language justifications for their quality assessments. The core of their approach is a *Score-based Instruction Generation (SIG)* pipeline that automatically generates a large-scale video quality-centric instruction dataset called *Score2Instruct (S2I)*. SIG avoids the need for expensive human annotations or proprietary systems by scoring various quality dimensions of unlabeled videos and then mapping these scores to text-defined levels. It uses a hierarchical Chain-of-Thought (CoT) approach to model the relationships between these quality dimensions and the overall perceived quality, mimicking the human visual system (HVS). The resulting S2I dataset is then used to instruction-tune video LMMs. The authors also create a new benchmark, *S2I-Bench*, to evaluate the quality justification capabilities of these LMMs. Experimental results demonstrate that the proposed method improves both quality scoring and justification performance across multiple video LMMs.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The key novelty lies in the *automated and scalable* data generation pipeline (SIG).  While adapting LMMs to VQA isn't entirely new, the traditional reliance on manual annotations or GPT-4 for instruction creation has been a significant bottleneck. SIG offers a practical solution to overcome this limitation. The hierarchical CoT and S2I-Bench, while incremental, contribute to a more robust evaluation and training process.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a crucial limitation of traditional VQA: the lack of explainability. By enabling LMMs to provide justifications, the work opens up new possibilities for understanding and improving video quality, which is essential in various applications, including video compression, streaming, and user-generated content. The S2I dataset and benchmark will likely become valuable resources for the community.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Scalable Data Generation:**  The SIG pipeline demonstrably reduces the reliance on expensive human annotations, enabling the creation of a large and diverse training dataset.
    *   **Improved Justification Ability:** The experiments show a clear improvement in the quality of justifications generated by the LMMs trained on S2I, making the VQA process more transparent and interpretable.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The S2I-Bench provides a better evaluation of the quality justification ability of VQA models.
    *   **Progressive Tuning Strategy:** The proposed two-stage tuning process seems to improve the quality justification ability of the LMM models.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Reliance on Expert Models for Scoring:** While the authors avoid human annotation for *instructions*, they still rely on expert models for scoring the quality dimensions. The performance of SIG and therefore the quality of S2I data will depend on the accuracy of these underlying models.
    *   **Generalizability:** The models are still evaluated on limited datasets. Though "in the wild," there is a question of how well these generalise beyond the available datasets.
    *   **Limited Analysis of Failure Cases:** The paper focuses on overall performance gains but offers little analysis of the types of videos or quality issues where the LMMs still struggle. A deeper dive into failure cases would provide valuable insights for future research.
    *   **Dependence on GPT for VCG Calculation:** While it is claimed that the metrics designed better capture video-specific aspects, GPT still needs to be prompted to calculate these, introducing a reliance on a proprietary tool.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to significantly influence the field by:

    *   **Promoting Explainable VQA:** Encouraging the development of VQA systems that provide not just scores but also explanations for their assessments.
    *   **Enabling New Applications:**  Facilitating applications that require a deeper understanding of video quality, such as adaptive video streaming and automated content moderation.
    *   **Accelerating Research:**  Providing a valuable dataset (S2I) and benchmark (S2I-Bench) for researchers working on VQA and multimodal learning.

**Score:** 7.5

**Justification:**

The paper presents a solid and significant contribution to the field of VQA. The SIG pipeline is a valuable technique for generating training data and enables the generation of more comprehensive evaluation metrics for VQA systems. The experimental results demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed approach. While there are weaknesses related to reliance on expert models for scoring, generalization, limited failure case analysis, and a reliance on GPT for VCG calculation, the overall impact of the paper on promoting explainable VQA and providing resources for the community is substantial. I believe this work has the potential to catalyze further research in this area.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### HybridQ: Hybrid Classical-Quantum Generative Adversarial Network for Skin Disease Image Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.21015v1)
- **Authors**: Qingyue Jiao, Kangyu Zheng, Yiyu Shi, Zhiding Liang
- **Abstract**: Machine learning-assisted diagnosis is gaining traction in skin disease detection, but training effective models requires large amounts of high-quality data. Skin disease datasets often suffer from class imbalance, privacy concerns, and object bias, making data augmentation essential. While classical generative models are widely used, they demand extensive computational resources and lengthy training time. Quantum computing offers a promising alternative, but existing quantum-based image generation methods can only yield grayscale low-quality images. Through a novel classical-quantum latent space fusion technique, our work overcomes this limitation and introduces the first classical-quantum generative adversarial network (GAN) capable of generating color medical images. Our model outperforms classical deep convolutional GANs and existing hybrid classical-quantum GANs in both image generation quality and classification performance boost when used as data augmentation. Moreover, the performance boost is comparable with that achieved using state-of-the-art classical generative models, yet with over 25 times fewer parameters and 10 times fewer training epochs. Such results suggest a promising future for quantum image generation as quantum hardware advances. Finally, we demonstrate the robust performance of our model on real IBM quantum machine with hardware noise.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "HybridQ: Hybrid Classical-Quantum Generative Adversarial Network for Skin Disease Image Generation":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces HybridQ, a novel hybrid classical-quantum generative adversarial network (GAN) designed to generate color medical images, specifically for skin disease diagnosis. Addressing the limitations of existing quantum-based image generation methods (which often produce low-quality, grayscale images), HybridQ employs a classical-quantum latent space fusion technique.  The method uses classical pre- and post-processing layers around quantum generators trained in latent space to improve scalability and mitigate noise, especially on Noisy Intermediate-Scale Quantum (NISQ) hardware. The paper demonstrates that HybridQ outperforms classical deep convolutional GANs and existing hybrid classical-quantum GANs (like MosaiQ) in image generation quality and classification performance when used for data augmentation. The authors show that their model requires significantly fewer parameters and less training time than state-of-the-art classical medical image generative models (like VQ-MAGE) while achieving comparable augmentation performance.  Finally, they demonstrate the model's robustness by running it on a real IBM quantum machine.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The primary novelty lies in its architecture and application.
    *   **Classical-Quantum Fusion:** The use of a hybrid classical-quantum GAN with a latent space-based training scheme is a valuable contribution.  It provides a way to mitigate noise in quantum generators.
    *   **Color Medical Image Generation:** Successfully generating color medical images using a hybrid quantum approach, where previous attempts were typically grayscale and low-resolution, is a significant step forward.
    *   **Parameter Efficiency:** Claiming significantly fewer parameters (25x) and less training time (10x) than VQ-MAGE, if true, is a noteworthy achievement. This is especially relevant for quantum computing, where resource constraints are a major challenge.

* **Significance:**

    *   **Medical Imaging Augmentation:** The ability to generate high-quality medical images has direct practical implications for data augmentation, particularly for skin disease datasets that suffer from class imbalance and privacy concerns.
    *   **Quantum Machine Learning Progress:**  The paper contributes to the ongoing effort to make quantum machine learning applicable in real-world scenarios.  Demonstrating robustness on real quantum hardware (even noisy hardware) is a crucial step.
    *   **NISQ-era Application:**  This work provides an example of how to leverage NISQ technology for a specific task. This is important because quantum computing is still in its early stages.

* **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly identifies the challenges in skin disease detection and the need for effective data augmentation.
    *   **Well-defined Methodology:**  The HybridQ architecture and training process are explained adequately.
    *   **Empirical Validation:** The experiments compare HybridQ to relevant baselines (DCGAN, MosaiQ, and VQ-MAGE) on standard datasets (ISIC 2019, Fitzpatrick17k).
    *   **Real Hardware Testing:**  Demonstrating the model on a real IBM quantum computer strengthens the work.

* **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Scale of Quantum Computation:** The quantum component, while novel, likely still performs a relatively small part of the overall image generation process due to the limitations of current quantum computers.  It might be premature to claim a significant speedup or efficiency gain solely based on the quantum part without a more detailed analysis of the computational costs on both classical and quantum components.
    *   **Quantum Advantage Debate:** The paper doesn't provide strong evidence of a "quantum advantage." The authors state performance "comparable" with VQ-MAGE but achieving this with significantly fewer parameters/time does not demonstrate any quantum advantage per se.
    *   **Reproducibility:** As with much quantum computing research, reproducibility can be an issue due to the limited access to quantum hardware and the complexity of the software stacks involved.
    *   **Generalizability:**  The focus on skin disease may limit the generalizability of the HybridQ architecture to other medical imaging domains, although the underlying principles might be adaptable.
    *   **Visual Quality:** While the authors claim improved image quality visually, the images presented lack very fine details and could be improved.

* **Potential Influence:**

    *   The paper could inspire further research into hybrid classical-quantum approaches for image generation, especially in medical imaging.
    *   The latent space fusion technique might be adopted and adapted in other quantum machine learning applications.
    *   It showcases how to handle limitations in NISQ devices.

**Rationale for Score:**

HybridQ presents a noteworthy advancement by successfully generating color medical images using a hybrid classical-quantum GAN. This, combined with claims of parameter efficiency and validation on real quantum hardware, positions it as a significant contribution. However, the work also needs to address the evidence regarding the true speedup on quantum computers and the overall performance. Given the relatively small scale of quantum computation, this approach's impact will grow significantly as quantum hardware matures.

Score: 6.5

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 6/10

### Instella-T2I: Pushing the Limits of 1D Discrete Latent Space Image Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.21022v1)
- **Authors**: Ze Wang, Hao Chen, Benran Hu, Jiang Liu, Ximeng Sun, Jialian Wu, Yusheng Su, Xiaodong Yu, Emad Barsoum, Zicheng Liu
- **Abstract**: Image tokenization plays a critical role in reducing the computational demands of modeling high-resolution images, significantly improving the efficiency of image and multimodal understanding and generation. Recent advances in 1D latent spaces have reduced the number of tokens required by eliminating the need for a 2D grid structure. In this paper, we further advance compact discrete image representation by introducing 1D binary image latents. By representing each image as a sequence of binary vectors, rather than using traditional one-hot codebook tokens, our approach preserves high-resolution details while maintaining the compactness of 1D latents. To the best of our knowledge, our text-to-image models are the first to achieve competitive performance in both diffusion and auto-regressive generation using just 128 discrete tokens for images up to 1024x1024, demonstrating up to a 32-fold reduction in token numbers compared to standard VQ-VAEs. The proposed 1D binary latent space, coupled with simple model architectures, achieves marked improvements in speed training and inference speed. Our text-to-image models allow for a global batch size of 4096 on a single GPU node with 8 AMD MI300X GPUs, and the training can be completed within 200 GPU days. Our models achieve competitive performance compared to modern image generation models without any in-house private training data or post-training refinements, offering a scalable and efficient alternative to conventional tokenization methods.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Instella-T2I: Pushing the Limits of 1D Discrete Latent Space Image Generation":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces "Instella-T2I," a novel approach to text-to-image generation that leverages a highly compact 1D binary latent space. Instead of using traditional 2D grids or one-hot codebook tokens, images are represented as sequences of binary vectors, preserving high-resolution details (up to 1024x1024) while drastically reducing the number of tokens required. The authors achieve competitive performance in both diffusion and auto-regressive generative models using only 128 discrete tokens.  This compact representation leads to significant improvements in training and inference speed, allowing for large batch sizes and faster generation times.  The paper emphasizes training efficiency on publicly available datasets, aiming for reproducible results without reliance on private data or extensive post-processing. They also use the open-source AMD OLMO-1B model as their text feature encoder.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The most compelling aspect of the paper is the 1D binary latent space representation. While 1D latent spaces have been explored before, representing *each token* as a binary vector rather than a single codebook entry is a key innovation. This enables a much more compact latent representation without sacrificing reconstruction quality.  The authors also focus on making training recipes reproducible by using open data sources and open-source models.
*   **Significance:** Reducing the number of tokens required for image generation is crucial for improving efficiency and scalability, especially in resource-constrained environments. The authors demonstrate a substantial reduction in token numbers (up to 32x compared to VQ-VAEs) which has implications for training time, inference speed, and memory footprint. This has significant potential for democratizing access to high-quality image generation.
*   **Strengths:**

    *   **High Compression & Quality:** The core technical contribution of representing images with a compact binary code while maintaining competitive generation quality is strong.
    *   **Efficiency:**  The gains in training and inference speed due to the reduced token count are significant and well-documented with benchmarks.
    *   **Reproducibility:**  The use of open-source components (OLMO-1B) and publicly available datasets enhances reproducibility and lowers the barrier to entry for research.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:**  The paper includes quantitative evaluations using standard metrics (CLIP score, ImageReward, GenEval) and qualitative comparisons against established models.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **FID Scores:** The paper acknowledges that the reported FID scores are not as competitive as some other models, suggesting that the model's diversity might be limited or that the binary latent space could benefit from further optimization for perceptual quality. This requires more future work in finding the best image generative process.
    *   **Reconstruction Artifacts:** The issue of artifacts, particularly in high-frequency regions, indicates there’s room for improvement in the decoder or in the tokenization process itself.
    *   **Generalization:** The tokenizer requires training on a fixed set of resolutions, limiting its generalization capabilities to arbitrary image sizes, which impacts deployment flexibility.
*   **Impact:** The paper's contribution lies in demonstrating the feasibility and benefits of ultra-compact image representations for text-to-image generation. This can pave the way for more efficient and accessible models. Specifically, the lower training cost opens up the possibility of training larger image-language models to broader audiences. This might benefit downstream tasks of visual representation learning, image and video editing, image understanding etc..

**Overall Assessment:**

The paper presents a valuable contribution to the field by demonstrating a highly efficient text-to-image generation framework based on a novel 1D binary latent space. While there are some limitations regarding reconstruction quality and generalization, the significant gains in efficiency and the emphasis on reproducibility make this work a worthwhile advancement. The potential to scale up these methods and integrate them into larger multimodal models is promising. However, the paper could be strengthened by addressing the identified limitations in future work.

**Score: 7.5**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### STEP Planner: Constructing cross-hierarchical subgoal tree as an embodied long-horizon task planner
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.21030v1)
- **Authors**: Zhou Tianxing, Wang Zhirui, Ao Haojia, Chen Guangyan, Xing Boyang, Cheng Jingwen, Yang Yi, Yue Yufeng
- **Abstract**: The ability to perform reliable long-horizon task planning is crucial for deploying robots in real-world environments. However, directly employing Large Language Models (LLMs) as action sequence generators often results in low success rates due to their limited reasoning ability for long-horizon embodied tasks. In the STEP framework, we construct a subgoal tree through a pair of closed-loop models: a subgoal decomposition model and a leaf node termination model. Within this framework, we develop a hierarchical tree structure that spans from coarse to fine resolutions. The subgoal decomposition model leverages a foundation LLM to break down complex goals into manageable subgoals, thereby spanning the subgoal tree. The leaf node termination model provides real-time feedback based on environmental states, determining when to terminate the tree spanning and ensuring each leaf node can be directly converted into a primitive action. Experiments conducted in both the VirtualHome WAH-NL benchmark and on real robots demonstrate that STEP achieves long-horizon embodied task completion with success rates up to 34% (WAH-NL) and 25% (real robot) outperforming SOTA methods.
- **Summary**: Okay, here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "STEP Planner: Constructing cross-hierarchical subgoal tree as an embodied long-horizon task planner," based on the provided text:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces STEP, a novel long-horizon task planner for embodied agents (robots). STEP addresses limitations in using Large Language Models (LLMs) directly for action sequence generation by building a hierarchical "subgoal tree." This tree decomposes complex tasks into manageable subgoals, spanning from coarse to fine resolutions. STEP uses a closed-loop system of two models: (1) a subgoal decomposition model that recursively breaks down tasks, and (2) a leaf node termination model that determines when a subgoal is sufficiently refined to be translated into a primitive action.  The paper claims STEP mitigates "contextual" and "logical" gaps that hinder LLM performance in long-horizon tasks, leading to superior results compared to existing methods in both simulated (VirtualHome) and real-world robot experiments.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies primarily in the specific combination of techniques rather than groundbreaking individual components. Hierarchical planning, task decomposition, and using LLMs for robotics are well-established concepts. The construction of the subgoal tree using a closed-loop system of decomposition and termination models represents an incremental advance, rather than a paradigm shift. A significant part of the innovation comes from addressing the identified “contextual” and “logical” gaps, and the explicit design to reduce these factors via the specific tree structure. Whether this architecture will lead to widespread adoption is uncertain, but its specific design with specific rationale shows novelty.

**Significance:**  The paper aims to tackle a significant problem in robotics: making LLMs effective for complex, long-duration tasks. The empirical results suggest STEP achieves better performance than existing approaches (SayCan, LoTa-Bench, ProgPrompt). The real-robot experiments add credibility, even if they are limited in scope (a few tasks with a single robot platform).

**Strengths:**

*   **Problem Definition:** Clearly articulates the "contextual" and "logical" gaps hindering LLM-based planning in embodied tasks.
*   **Method Design:** The subgoal tree structure and the closed-loop decomposition/termination models are well-motivated by the problem definition.
*   **Empirical Evaluation:** Includes experiments in both simulation (VirtualHome) and real-world robotics, providing evidence for the effectiveness of STEP. The use of an ablation study is also commendable, helping to isolate the contributions of different components.
*   **Detailed Experimental Analysis:** Results Analysis is conducted to explain why STEP can deal with long-horizon tasks.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Incremental Nature:** The approach builds upon existing techniques rather than introducing radically new concepts.
*   **LLM Reliance:** STEP still relies on LLMs as the core reasoning engine within the decomposition and termination models. Therefore, STEP may not be able to scale to more complex environments.
*   **Limited Real-World Experiments:**  Experiments are restricted to a few specific tasks and a single robot platform. More diverse robotic platforms and task sets are needed. Also, while real-world experiments are present, more data is needed on the error types.
*   **Lack of detailed comparison:** A more thorough comparison to related tree-based methods (e.g., specific ToT variants adapted for robotics) would strengthen the evaluation.

**Potential Influence:** The paper could influence future research in LLM-based robotics by:

*   Highlighting the importance of addressing contextual and logical gaps in long-horizon task planning.
*   Providing a practical and effective approach to task decomposition using subgoal trees.
*   Inspiring the development of more sophisticated leaf node termination models to improve action grounding.

**Justification for Score:**

Given the paper's strengths and weaknesses, I would assign it a score of **7/10**.

While the paper offers a well-designed and empirically validated approach to a significant problem, its incremental nature and the continued reliance on LLMs prevent it from being a truly groundbreaking contribution. The experimental results are promising, but the limitations in real-world testing and comparisons to related work hold it back from a higher score.
Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.RO
- **Score**: 7/10

### Large Language Models Acing Chartered Accountancy
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.21031v1)
- **Authors**: Jatin Gupta, Akhil Sharma, Saransh Singhania, Mohammad Adnan, Sakshi Deo, Ali Imam Abidi, Keshav Gupta
- **Abstract**: Advanced intelligent systems, particularly Large Language Models (LLMs), are significantly reshaping financial practices through advancements in Natural Language Processing (NLP). However, the extent to which these models effectively capture and apply domain-specific financial knowledge remains uncertain. Addressing a critical gap in the expansive Indian financial context, this paper introduces CA-Ben, a Chartered Accountancy benchmark specifically designed to evaluate the financial, legal, and quantitative reasoning capabilities of LLMs. CA-Ben comprises structured question-answer datasets derived from the rigorous examinations conducted by the Institute of Chartered Accountants of India (ICAI), spanning foundational, intermediate, and advanced CA curriculum stages. Six prominent LLMs i.e. GPT 4o, LLAMA 3.3 70B, LLAMA 3.1 405B, MISTRAL Large, Claude 3.5 Sonnet, and Microsoft Phi 4 were evaluated using standardized protocols. Results indicate variations in performance, with Claude 3.5 Sonnet and GPT-4o outperforming others, especially in conceptual and legal reasoning. Notable challenges emerged in numerical computations and legal interpretations. The findings emphasize the strengths and limitations of current LLMs, suggesting future improvements through hybrid reasoning and retrieval-augmented generation methods, particularly for quantitative analysis and accurate legal interpretation.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces CA-Ben, a new benchmark specifically designed to evaluate Large Language Models (LLMs) in the context of Indian Chartered Accountancy (CA). The benchmark comprises question-answer datasets derived from the Institute of Chartered Accountants of India (ICAI) examinations across foundation, intermediate, and final levels.  The authors tested six LLMs (GPT-40, LLAMA 3.3 70B, LLAMA 3.1 405B, MISTRAL Large, Claude 3.5 Sonnet, and Microsoft Phi 4) on CA-Ben and found variations in performance. Claude 3.5 Sonnet and GPT-40 generally outperformed the others, particularly in conceptual and legal reasoning, while all models struggled with numerical computations and legal interpretation. The paper concludes that LLMs have potential in finance but require improvements, particularly in quantitative analysis and legal interpretation, suggesting future work could focus on hybrid reasoning and retrieval-augmented generation methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Strengths:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper addresses a critical gap by introducing a domain-specific benchmark, CA-Ben, for evaluating LLMs within the Indian financial context. This is a significant contribution because finance is complex and requires specialist understanding and there is a lack of datasets for evaluation in this specific domain. The focus on Indian Chartered Accountancy makes it more specific.
*   **Relevance:**  Given the increasing adoption of AI in finance, evaluating LLMs' capabilities in this area is crucial. A good benchmark is vital for further research. The paper is important for assessing the readiness and potential of AI tools in financial tasks and regulatory content.
*   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper tests a range of state-of-the-art LLMs (GPT-40, LLAMA 3.3 70B, LLAMA 3.1 405B, MISTRAL Large, Claude 3.5 Sonnet, and Microsoft Phi 4) at varying levels of complexity in the chartered accountancy domain.
*   **Detailed Analysis:** The paper provides a breakdown of performance across different levels (Foundation, Intermediate, Final) and subjects within the CA curriculum. This granular analysis highlights the specific strengths and weaknesses of each LLM.
*   **Clear Methodology:** The paper clearly outlines the benchmark design, experimental setup (system prompt, parameter tuning, testing pipeline), and evaluation metrics.  The use of a standardized prompt and a defined regex extraction method improves the reproducibility of results.
*   **Actionable Insights:** The paper's findings provide specific areas for improvement, such as numerical computation and legal interpretation. It also suggests future research directions (hybrid reasoning, RAG).

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Dataset Size:** The paper doesn't explicitly state the total number of QA pairs in the CA-Ben benchmark. A larger dataset would provide more robust and generalizable results. While the examinations the questions are based on are rigorous, the specific questions included may be a small sample.
*   **Complexity of Questions:** Chartered Accountancy questions often require nuanced understanding and practical judgment, something difficult to quantify in the QA format. The paper doesn't discuss strategies for handling questions requiring more qualitative reasoning, making assumptions that a simple, multiple choice is sufficient.
*   **Narrow Scope:** While focusing on Indian Chartered Accountancy adds novelty, it also limits the scope. The benchmark's generalizability to other financial contexts (e.g., different accounting standards, legal systems) is unclear.
*   **Statistical Significance:**  The paper does not address the statistical significance of the differences in performance between the models. Without such analysis, it's difficult to conclude whether the observed differences are meaningful or simply due to random variation.
*   **Regex as a Limitation:** Relying solely on a Regex to extract answers may have led to some inaccuracies in performance evaluation. Regex, though simple, are not always the most effective at reliably extracting information.

**Overall:**

The paper makes a worthwhile contribution by introducing a new benchmark, CA-Ben, in the under-explored domain of Indian Chartered Accountancy. The comprehensive evaluation of multiple LLMs and the clear articulation of findings are definite strengths. The dataset is relatively new, so these limitations could be addressed in the next iteration of this paper. However, the limitations regarding the dataset size, complexity of the questions, and lack of statistical validation limit the overall impact.

**Score: 6.5**

**Rationale:** The paper fills a crucial gap by providing a new benchmark for a specific financial domain. However, the limitations in dataset size, and lack of statistical significance analysis restrain its overall contribution. Given the need to validate and expand the dataset, the contribution can be considered good, but has room for significant improvement and validation.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### RecCoT: Enhancing Recommendation via Chain-of-Thought
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.21032v1)
- **Authors**: Shuo Yang, Jiangxia Cao, Haipeng Li, Yuqi Mao, Shuchao Pang
- **Abstract**: In real-world applications, users always interact with items in multiple aspects, such as through implicit binary feedback (e.g., clicks, dislikes, long views) and explicit feedback (e.g., comments, reviews). Modern recommendation systems (RecSys) learn user-item collaborative signals from these implicit feedback signals as a large-scale binary data-streaming, subsequently recommending other highly similar items based on users' personalized historical interactions. However, from this collaborative-connection perspective, the RecSys does not focus on the actual content of the items themselves but instead prioritizes higher-probability signals of behavioral co-occurrence among items. Consequently, under this binary learning paradigm, the RecSys struggles to understand why a user likes or dislikes certain items. To alleviate it, some works attempt to utilize the content-based reviews to capture the semantic knowledge to enhance recommender models. However, most of these methods focus on predicting the ratings of reviews, but do not provide a human-understandable explanation.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper, "RecCoT: Enhancing Recommendation via Chain-of-Thought":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces RecCoT, a novel recommendation framework designed to improve the understanding of user preferences by leveraging Chain-of-Thought (CoT) reasoning. It addresses the limitations of traditional collaborative filtering methods, which primarily focus on probability calculations and struggle to interpret negative user behaviors. RecCoT uses a two-step approach: (1) a larger LLM generates interpretable CoTs for reviews, supervised by a frequency-aware reward policy to adapt to long-tail rating distributions; (2) a smaller LLM is fine-tuned to produce semantic embeddings based on the CoTs, which are then used to enhance recommendation accuracy.  The paper claims significant improvements in recommendation performance compared to state-of-the-art methods, attributing it to the enhanced understanding of user preferences achieved through CoT reasoning.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a potentially novel approach by integrating CoT reasoning into the recommendation process. The idea of generating human-interpretable explanations for user reviews to improve recommendation accuracy is interesting. The frequency-aware reward policy to address the long-tail problem is a well-motivated contribution. The two-step approach of using a larger LLM for CoT generation and a smaller LLM for semantic embedding attempts to balance accuracy and computational efficiency.
*   **Significance:**  If the claims of significant performance improvements are well-supported, RecCoT could have a notable impact on the field. The ability to provide more interpretable recommendations is valuable for building user trust and satisfaction. The approach of using CoT to overcome the limitations of traditional collaborative filtering methods is relevant to current research trends.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   The problem of interpretability in recommendation systems is well-defined and relevant.
    *   The proposed RecCoT framework is a logical approach to address the identified problem.
    *   The frequency-aware reward policy is a practical solution to mitigate reward hacking issues caused by the long-tail distribution in ratings data.
    *   The experimental results demonstrate improvements over existing state-of-the-art methods. The analysis of user review length and its impact is helpful.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Lack of implementation and training details**: The paper does not describe with enough detail the training procedure and hyperparameter optimization, especially the parameters of the frequency-aware loss. The precise values of lambdas, mus, y and K are not provided.
    *   **Ablation study**: Although it contains a basic ablation study by removing CoT information, the paper would benefit from further analysis of the contribution of each component, especially the frequency-aware loss.
    *   **Dataset issues**: The Amazon datasets, while standard, may have inherent biases that could skew the results.
    *   **Generalizability concerns**: The performance improvements are demonstrated on Amazon datasets. The effectiveness of RecCoT on other types of recommendation tasks or datasets is not clear.
    *   **Clarity Issues:** While the overall framework is clear, certain parts of the methodology (especially regarding the optimization process) could benefit from improved clarity and more specific implementation details.
    *   **Computational Cost:** Although the authors attempt to make it more computationally efficient by using a smaller LLM for the semantic alignment part, the initial step of CoT generation with a large LLM will still be computationally demanding, especially for large-scale datasets.

**Overall:**

The paper presents an interesting and potentially significant contribution to the field of recommendation systems. The idea of incorporating CoT reasoning to improve recommendation interpretability and accuracy is compelling. The reported performance improvements are promising, but concerns about generalizability and the impact of potential biases in the datasets used in the experiments need to be carefully addressed in future work.

**Score: 6.5**

**Rationale:**

The RecCoT framework offers a genuinely interesting and novel approach with the inclusion of interpretable reasoning. The authors have addressed a clearly defined problem and, through the integration of a reward-constrained learning mechanism for large language models (LLMs), provide a robust solution. Furthermore, the utilization of a lightweight small language model for downstream recommender systems contributes to the computational efficiency, making it more suitable for use in environments that have resource constraints. Nonetheless, the paper could be substantially enhanced by including more implementation and training details, especially the parameters of the frequency-aware loss, as well as further analysis in ablation studies to demonstrate the contribution of individual components.

- **Classification**: cs.IR
- **Score**: 6/10

### BLOCKS: Blockchain-supported Cross-Silo Knowledge Sharing for Efficient LLM Services
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.21033v1)
- **Authors**: Zhaojiacheng Zhou, Hongze Liu, Shijing Yuan, Hanning Zhang, Jiong Lou, Chentao Wu, Jie Li
- **Abstract**: The hallucination problem of Large Language Models (LLMs) has increasingly drawn attention. Augmenting LLMs with external knowledge is a promising solution to address this issue. However, due to privacy and security concerns, a vast amount of downstream task-related knowledge remains dispersed and isolated across various "silos," making it difficult to access. To bridge this knowledge gap, we propose a blockchain-based external knowledge framework that coordinates multiple knowledge silos to provide reliable foundational knowledge for large model retrieval while ensuring data security. Technically, we distill knowledge from local data into prompts and execute transactions and records on the blockchain. Additionally, we introduce a reputation mechanism and cross-validation to ensure knowledge quality and provide incentives for participation. Furthermore, we design a query generation framework that provides a direct API interface for large model retrieval. To evaluate the performance of our proposed framework, we conducted extensive experiments on various knowledge sources. The results demonstrate that the proposed framework achieves efficient LLM service knowledge sharing in blockchain environments.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper proposes BLOCKS, a blockchain-based framework for cross-silo knowledge sharing to enhance Large Language Model (LLM) services. BLOCKS addresses the challenge of accessing siloed task-relevant knowledge by coordinating multiple silos while preserving data security and addressing incentive, quality, and security concerns.  Key components include a smart contract-based reputation mechanism, a benchmark-based validation process, a knowledge distillation method converting local data into prompts, and a caching mechanism called PROCache to improve retrieval efficiency. The framework is implemented using Cosmos and evaluated across various knowledge sources, demonstrating improved LLM service knowledge sharing.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in the specific combination of blockchain technology, reputation mechanisms, and caching strategies specifically tailored for LLM knowledge augmentation in a distributed, multi-silo setting. Existing blockchain-based knowledge sharing systems haven't directly addressed the specific challenges and requirements of LLM services, particularly the need for prompt generation, fast retrieval, and robust defense against prompt injection attacks. While the individual components (blockchain, reputation, caching) are not entirely novel on their own, their integration within a coherent framework targeting LLMs makes this contribution worthwhile.

*   **Significance:** Addressing the knowledge silo problem is crucial for improving LLM accuracy and reliability, especially in domains requiring specialized knowledge. BLOCKS offers a potential solution for unlocking valuable, domain-specific knowledge that would otherwise remain inaccessible. The proposed reputation system and validation process are significant because they try to address the critical security vulnerabilities related to untrusted data and prompt injection attacks, which often plague distributed systems.  The experimental results, while seemingly preliminary, suggest that the framework effectively balances knowledge quality, retrieval efficiency, and security.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   The paper identifies a critical problem hindering LLM performance and applicability.
    *   The proposed architecture is well-defined and logically structured, with clear explanations of each component's function.
    *   The integration of blockchain technology offers a promising solution for addressing trust and incentive challenges in distributed knowledge sharing.
    *   PROCache represents a practical improvement to address the throughput limitations inherent in blockchain.
    *   Security considerations are explicitly addressed with a focus on reputation and validation mechanisms.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The experimental evaluation, while promising, could be more extensive. More details about the specific datasets, evaluation metrics, and experimental setup would strengthen the paper. The experiments are based on simulating all the blockchain agents in docker containers, it would have been great to see this tested on a real-world multi-node environment.
    *   The paper could benefit from a more detailed comparison with existing knowledge sharing systems and LLM augmentation techniques, highlighting the advantages and limitations of BLOCKS in specific use cases.
    *   The economic model, particularly regarding incentive distribution and fee structures, could be explored in greater depth.
    *   The discussion of real-world deployment challenges, such as scalability and regulatory compliance, is limited.

*   **Potential Influence:** If further validated with more comprehensive experiments, BLOCKS could influence the design of future knowledge sharing platforms for LLMs. It provides a valuable blueprint for building secure, incentivized, and efficient systems for accessing distributed knowledge. It will be interesting to see how the blockchain structure can impact LLMs efficiency and cost.

**Score: 7**

**Rationale:** The paper presents a novel integration of blockchain, reputation, and caching techniques to address a significant challenge in LLM deployment: accessing siloed knowledge. While the individual components are not entirely groundbreaking, the specific application to LLMs and the focus on security make it a valuable contribution. The paper has some limitations in terms of experiment, which needs more real-world results. The ideas presented will influence future research in secure and distributed LLM augmentation.

- **Classification**: cs.DC
- **Score**: 7/10

### DidSee: Diffusion-Based Depth Completion for Material-Agnostic Robotic Perception and Manipulation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.21034v1)
- **Authors**: Wenzhou Lyu, Jialing Lin, Wenqi Ren, Ruihao Xia, Feng Qian, Yang Tang
- **Abstract**: Commercial RGB-D cameras often produce noisy, incomplete depth maps for non-Lambertian objects. Traditional depth completion methods struggle to generalize due to the limited diversity and scale of training data. Recent advances exploit visual priors from pre-trained text-to-image diffusion models to enhance generalization in dense prediction tasks. However, we find that biases arising from training-inference mismatches in the vanilla diffusion framework significantly impair depth completion performance. Additionally, the lack of distinct visual features in non-Lambertian regions further hinders precise prediction. To address these issues, we propose \textbf{DidSee}, a diffusion-based framework for depth completion on non-Lambertian objects. First, we integrate a rescaled noise scheduler enforcing a zero terminal signal-to-noise ratio to eliminate signal leakage bias. Second, we devise a noise-agnostic single-step training formulation to alleviate error accumulation caused by exposure bias and optimize the model with a task-specific loss. Finally, we incorporate a semantic enhancer that enables joint depth completion and semantic segmentation, distinguishing objects from backgrounds and yielding precise, fine-grained depth maps. DidSee achieves state-of-the-art performance on multiple benchmarks, demonstrates robust real-world generalization, and effectively improves downstream tasks such as category-level pose estimation and robotic grasping.Project page: https://wenzhoulyu.github.io/DidSee/
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the DidSee paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the problem of depth completion for non-Lambertian objects, where commercial RGB-D cameras often produce noisy and incomplete data. The authors propose DidSee, a diffusion-based depth completion framework leveraging the visual priors of pre-trained text-to-image diffusion models like Stable Diffusion. DidSee tackles biases inherent in vanilla diffusion frameworks, specifically signal leakage bias and exposure bias. It incorporates a rescaled noise scheduler enforcing a zero terminal signal-to-noise ratio to eliminate signal leakage and a noise-agnostic single-step training formulation to mitigate exposure bias. A semantic enhancer is also introduced for joint depth completion and semantic segmentation, improving object-background distinction. The paper demonstrates state-of-the-art performance on multiple benchmarks and showcases its effectiveness in downstream tasks like category-level pose estimation and robotic grasping.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in several aspects:

    *   **Bias Identification and Mitigation:**  The systematic analysis of signal leakage and exposure biases in the context of diffusion-based depth completion is a valuable contribution. While these biases might be known in the broader diffusion literature, their specific impact and mitigation strategy tailored for dense prediction are novel and practically relevant.
    *   **Zero Terminal-SNR Rescaled Scheduler:** Adapting the noise scheduler to enforce a zero terminal-SNR to eliminate signal leakage bias. While the concept of a rescaled noise scheduler itself might not be brand new, the application and demonstration of its significance in *depth completion* tasks, where accuracy is paramount, is a distinct contribution.
    *   **Noise-Agnostic Single-Step Training:**  The single-step training formulation to address exposure bias is a clever adaptation.  This efficiently combines training with a specific task loss function without the complications of multi-step approaches, which can introduce conflicts.
    *   **Semantic Enhancer:**  The addition of the semantic enhancer, which improves object-background differentiation, is another novel element, especially tailored for handling transparent and reflective surfaces where visual cues are ambiguous. Using the color palette technique is a useful workaround for integrating semantic labels into the diffusion process.

*   **Significance:** The paper has significant practical implications for robotics and computer vision:

    *   **Improved Depth Completion:** The improved accuracy and robustness of depth completion, especially for non-Lambertian objects, directly benefit robotic tasks such as scene understanding, 3D reconstruction, and manipulation.
    *   **Material-Agnostic Perception:**  By addressing the challenges posed by transparent and reflective objects, the paper contributes to more robust and material-agnostic robotic perception. This is crucial for robots operating in diverse and unstructured real-world environments.
    *   **Downstream Task Performance:**  The tangible improvements in category-level pose estimation and robotic grasping demonstrate the practical value of the approach.
    * **Real-World Generalization**: Demonstrates robustness across diverse scenarios.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper presents thorough quantitative and qualitative results across multiple datasets and downstream tasks.
    *   **Ablation Studies:** The ablation studies clearly demonstrate the contributions of each component of the proposed framework.
    *   **Clear Writing:** The paper is well-written and clearly explains the proposed method and its advantages.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Computational Cost:** As acknowledged by the authors, the reliance on Stable Diffusion introduces a significant computational overhead. Addressing this limitation is crucial for practical deployment. The method is computationally expensive compared to non diffusion alternatives.
    *   **Incremental Advance:** While each component is novel in its application to this specific task, the individual pieces are built on existing concepts in diffusion models.  The innovation is more in the engineering and application of these ideas.
* **Limitations addressed:**
    * The paper addresses the computational cost in the conclusion providing future works.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper makes a solid contribution to depth completion for non-Lambertian objects. While the individual components might not be groundbreaking in isolation, their combined application and adaptation to address specific biases in diffusion-based depth completion is novel and yields significant practical improvements. The thorough evaluation and clear presentation further strengthen the paper. The identified weaknesses, particularly the computational cost, are important areas for future research, but they do not diminish the overall value of the contribution. The impact on robotics by improved grasping makes this a significant paper.

- **Score**: 8/10

### Little By Little: Continual Learning via Self-Activated Sparse Mixture-of-Rank Adaptive Learning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.21035v1)
- **Authors**: Haodong Lu, Chongyang Zhao, Jason Xue, Lina Yao, Kristen Moore, Dong Gong
- **Abstract**: Continual learning (CL) with large pre-trained models is challenged by catastrophic forgetting and task interference. Existing LoRA-based Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) approaches mitigate forgetting by assigning and freezing task-specific adapters, but suffer from interference, redundancy, and ambiguous routing due to coarse adapter-level selection. However, this design introduces three key challenges: 1) Interference: Activating full LoRA experts per input leads to subspace interference and prevents selective reuse of useful components across tasks. 2) Redundancy: Newly added experts often duplicate or contradict existing knowledge due to unnecessary activation of unrelated ranks and insufficient reuse of relevant ones. 3) Ambiguity: Overlapping features across tasks confuse the router, resulting in unstable expert assignments. As more experts accumulate, earlier task routing degrades, accelerating forgetting. We propose MoRA, a Mixture-of-Rank Adaptive learning approach with self-activated and sparse rank activation for CL. Unlike mixing multiple low-rank matrices, MoRA decomposes each rank-r update into r rank-1 components, each treated as an independent expert, enabling fine-grained mixture of rank-1 expert utilization while mitigating interference and redundancy. To avoid ambiguous routing, we propose that each rank-1 expert can infer its own relevance via intermediate activations. Coupled with our proposed rank pruning and activation budgets, MoRA adaptively selects a sparse mixture of ranks per input. We validate MoRA on continual learning tasks with CLIP and large language models (LLMs), analyzing both in-domain learning and out-of-domain forgetting/generalization during fine-tuning. MoRA shows significant effectiveness on enhancing CL with PTMs, and improving generalization while mitigating forgetting.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Little By Little: Continual Learning via Self-Activated Sparse Mixture-of-Rank Adaptive Learning (MoRA)":

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the challenges of continual learning (CL) with large pre-trained models (PTMs), specifically catastrophic forgetting and task interference. It identifies limitations of existing LoRA-based Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) approaches, including interference between experts, redundancy in learned knowledge, and ambiguity in routing. To mitigate these issues, the paper proposes MoRA, a novel approach that decomposes each rank-r LoRA update into r rank-1 components, treating each component as an independent expert. MoRA introduces a self-activation mechanism where each rank-1 expert infers its relevance to the input, promoting sparse rank activation and reducing routing ambiguity. The method also uses rank pruning and activation budgets. Experiments on CL tasks with CLIP and LLMs demonstrate MoRA's effectiveness in enhancing CL, improving generalization, and mitigating forgetting.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Novelty:** The key novelty lies in decomposing LoRA updates to the rank-1 level and using a self-activation mechanism for sparse expert selection. This fine-grained approach differentiates it from existing MoE-LoRA methods that operate at the adapter level. The self-activation mechanism also eliminates the need for a separately trained router, simplifying the architecture and potentially improving stability. The rank-pruning strategy is interesting and useful for further mitigating forgetting.
    *   **Technical Soundness:** The paper's methodology appears technically sound. The mathematical formulations are clear, and the proposed architecture is well-defined. The associative memory view of low-rank adaptation provides a valuable perspective. The experiment setup, including the comparison with strong baselines across multiple benchmarks and the inclusion of various ablation studies is extensive and rigorous.
    *   **Empirical Validation:** The experimental results convincingly demonstrate MoRA's superiority over existing CL methods for PTMs on CLIP and LLMs. The significant performance gains, reduced forgetting, and improved generalization are compelling evidence of the method's effectiveness. Visualizations of rank activations provide valuable insights into the model's behavior.
    *   **Clarity:** The paper is generally well-written and easy to follow, with clear explanations of the method and its benefits. The figures and tables are helpful in visualizing the concepts and results.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Hyperparameter Sensitivity:** The self-activation mechanism depends on several hyperparameters, such as the temperature, activation budget, and threshold. The paper does not thoroughly analyze the sensitivity of MoRA's performance to these hyperparameters, which could limit its practical applicability. Further detailed experiments demonstrating the hyperparameter sensitivity, and/or providing a procedure for selecting them, would be extremely beneficial.
    *   **Scalability Concerns:** While the proposed method performs strongly, the individual processing and selection of rank-1 "experts" could bring scalability challenges for large models. The paper doesn't provide a thorough discussion of computation cost comparison. A more comprehensive analysis including a report of the training time with/without MORA could be included.
    *   **Limited Theoretical Analysis:** The paper lacks a rigorous theoretical analysis of MoRA's properties. While the empirical results are strong, a theoretical understanding of why MoRA works would strengthen the paper's contribution.

*   **Significance:**

    *   MoRA significantly improves the performance of continual learning on PTMs, addressing the critical problem of catastrophic forgetting. The proposed method is efficient, scalable, and easy to implement. The associative memory view and the self-activation mechanism provide a novel and insightful perspective on low-rank adaptation. The gains are significant on established benchmarks, and analysis of retaining pre-trained knowledge is beneficial for future investigations.

**Justification for Score:**

Overall, the paper presents a significant contribution to the field of continual learning. The proposed MoRA method is novel, technically sound, empirically validated, and has the potential to influence future research in this area. Although there are some weaknesses regarding parameter sensitivity, scalability analysis, and theoretical understanding, the strengths of the paper outweigh the weaknesses. For these reasons, the manuscript deserves a strong score.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 8/10

### Boosting Domain Generalized and Adaptive Detection with Diffusion Models: Fitness, Generalization, and Transferability
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.21042v1)
- **Authors**: Boyong He, Yuxiang Ji, Zhuoyue Tan, Liaoni Wu
- **Abstract**: Detectors often suffer from performance drop due to domain gap between training and testing data. Recent methods explore diffusion models applied to domain generalization (DG) and adaptation (DA) tasks, but still struggle with large inference costs and have not yet fully leveraged the capabilities of diffusion models. We propose to tackle these problems by extracting intermediate features from a single-step diffusion process, improving feature collection and fusion to reduce inference time by 75% while enhancing performance on source domains (i.e., Fitness). Then, we construct an object-centered auxiliary branch by applying box-masked images with class prompts to extract robust and domain-invariant features that focus on object. We also apply consistency loss to align the auxiliary and ordinary branch, balancing fitness and generalization while preventing overfitting and improving performance on target domains (i.e., Generalization). Furthermore, within a unified framework, standard detectors are guided by diffusion detectors through feature-level and object-level alignment on source domains (for DG) and unlabeled target domains (for DA), thereby improving cross-domain detection performance (i.e., Transferability). Our method achieves competitive results on 3 DA benchmarks and 5 DG benchmarks. Additionally, experiments on COCO generalization benchmark demonstrate that our method maintains significant advantages and show remarkable efficiency in large domain shifts and low-data scenarios. Our work shows the superiority of applying diffusion models to domain generalized and adaptive detection tasks and offers valuable insights for visual perception tasks across diverse domains. The code is available at \href{https://github.com/heboyong/Fitness-Generalization-Transferability}{Fitness-Generalization-Transferability}.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper proposes a method to improve domain generalization (DG) and domain adaptation (DA) for object detection using diffusion models. The method tackles computational inefficiency of existing diffusion-based approaches by extracting intermediate features from a single-step diffusion process and using an optimized feature collection/fusion structure. It further introduces an object-centered auxiliary branch that leverages class prompts to extract robust, domain-invariant features. A consistency loss is applied to align the auxiliary and ordinary branches, balancing fitness (performance on source domains) and generalization. Finally, the method proposes a unified transfer framework to guide standard detectors using the improved diffusion detector at both feature and object levels, applicable to both DG and DA tasks. The paper demonstrates competitive results on several DG and DA benchmarks, and analyzes performance on a larger-scale COCO generalization benchmark.

**Critical Evaluation:**

The paper presents a reasonable contribution to the field of domain generalization and adaptation in object detection. Its novelty lies primarily in the specific architectural and training strategies employed to enhance diffusion model usage for DG/DA tasks while improving efficiency. Here's a breakdown of strengths and weaknesses:

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Efficiency Improvement:** The single-step feature extraction and optimized fusion significantly reduces the inference time, which is a practical and important consideration. This addresses a key limitation of previous diffusion-based methods.
    *   **Object-Centered Auxiliary Branch:**  Leveraging the multi-modal capabilities of diffusion models through class prompts is a novel idea and appears to improve feature representation.
    *   **Unified Transfer Framework:** The proposed unified framework enables better performance in cross-domain scenarios.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper includes extensive experimental results on multiple DG and DA benchmarks, along with an analysis of a larger-scale COCO generalization task.
    *   **Error Analysis:** The error analysis using TIDE provides insights into where the method excels and where there is room for improvement.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Incremental Novelty:** While the individual components (feature extraction strategy, auxiliary branch, consistency loss) contribute to the overall performance, the core idea of using diffusion models for DG/DA isn't entirely novel, building upon existing methods like DDT and GDD. The paper uses established techniques and carefully combines them for better results.
    *   **Dependency on Foundation Models:** The approach relies on pre-trained diffusion models (Stable Diffusion), which means the performance is inherently limited by the capabilities of these foundation models. While understandable, it reduces the novelty of the proposed approach.
    *   **Limited Gains in Some Scenarios:** The paper acknowledges that the gains achieved in Real to Artistic scenarios were limited, pointing out that frameworks with source-only domain alignment may not address tasks where target domains have considerable domain gaps. This highlights limitations of the proposed system.

*   **Significance:**

    *   The work addresses a practical problem (efficiency) in applying diffusion models to DG/DA tasks.
    *   It presents a strong empirical evaluation, showing consistent improvements over existing methods.
    *   The object-centered auxiliary branch could inspire further research into leveraging multimodal capabilities of diffusion models.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper represents a solid engineering contribution with some degree of novelty, successfully improving upon existing diffusion-based DG/DA methods. The efficiency gains are particularly valuable. While the core idea of using diffusion models isn't new, the specific architectural and training strategies are well-motivated and lead to tangible improvements. The comprehensive experiments and analyses bolster the paper's claims. However, the lack of disruptive innovation and dependency on pre-trained foundation models prevent it from achieving a higher score.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Improving Diffusion-Based Image Editing Faithfulness via Guidance and Scheduling
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2506.21045v1)
- **Authors**: Hansam Cho, Seoung Bum Kim
- **Abstract**: Text-guided diffusion models have become essential for high-quality image synthesis, enabling dynamic image editing. In image editing, two crucial aspects are editability, which determines the extent of modification, and faithfulness, which reflects how well unaltered elements are preserved. However, achieving optimal results is challenging because of the inherent trade-off between editability and faithfulness. To address this, we propose Faithfulness Guidance and Scheduling (FGS), which enhances faithfulness with minimal impact on editability. FGS incorporates faithfulness guidance to strengthen the preservation of input image information and introduces a scheduling strategy to resolve misalignment between editability and faithfulness. Experimental results demonstrate that FGS achieves superior faithfulness while maintaining editability. Moreover, its compatibility with various editing methods enables precise, high-quality image edits across diverse tasks.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Improving Diffusion-Based Image Editing Faithfulness via Guidance and Scheduling":

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the inherent trade-off between editability and faithfulness in text-guided diffusion models for image editing. It proposes a novel approach called Faithfulness Guidance and Scheduling (FGS). FGS incorporates "faithfulness guidance" to strengthen the preservation of input image information by perturbing transferred information from the reconstruction path and injecting it back into the editing path.  A "scheduling strategy" resolves misalignment between editability and faithfulness during the denoising process, adjusting the emphasis between these two goals across timesteps.  Experimental results demonstrate that FGS improves faithfulness while maintaining editability compared to existing methods and is compatible with various editing techniques.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in the specific combination of faithfulness guidance with a scheduling strategy within the diffusion model editing framework. While individual components such as guidance and manipulating attention have been explored before, the approach of perturbing transferred information *specifically* for the purpose of enhancing faithfulness in *image editing*, combined with a carefully designed scheduling scheme, seems relatively novel. It distinguishes itself from other attention manipulation approaches that primarily focus on guiding image synthesis in general. However, it builds on previous works on guidance and attention manipulation such as PAG, SEG and classifier-free guidance.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a crucial problem in the field of diffusion-based image editing: the difficulty in balancing editability with faithfulness.  By achieving improved faithfulness without significant loss in editability, FGS makes a meaningful contribution. This is valuable for applications where preserving key aspects of the original image is essential during editing. The demonstrated compatibility with several existing editing methods (P2P, MasaCtrl, StyleAligned, InfEdit) further increases the significance, suggesting FGS can serve as a general plug-in module. The quantitative and qualitative results presented seem compelling.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear problem definition and motivation.
    *   Well-explained technical approach with a logical flow.
    *   Thorough experimental evaluation, including quantitative and qualitative comparisons.
    *   Demonstrated generalizability across multiple existing editing methods.
    *   Ablation studies provide insight into the contribution of different components of FGS.
    *   The inclusion of a limitations section provides transparency.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The specific choice of Gaussian blur for perturbation (while justified through ablation) might not be the *most* optimal. A more sophisticated approach might be considered.
    *   The paper's reliance on PIE-Bench is good for comparison, but the PIE-Bench metrics themselves have limitations. While the paper uses both whole and unedited area SD for a better evaluation, reliance on CLIP for editability evaluation can be questionable.
    *   The memory usage and increased inference time, although manageable, could be a barrier in some applications and deserve more in-depth analysis or future work to mitigate.
    *   Some of the limitations are somewhat vaguely addressed (e.g., disentanglement of transferred information). Further investigation or potential solutions could be discussed.
    *   The approach is somewhat incremental, building on top of existing techniques.

*   **Potential Influence:**
    The paper is likely to influence future research in diffusion-based image editing, particularly for tasks that prioritize faithful modifications. The core idea of perturbing transferred information could inspire other techniques for targeted control of editing behavior. The modularity of FGS suggests it could be widely adopted.

*   **Justification of score:**

Given the above analysis, the paper presents a solid contribution to the field. While it builds upon existing techniques and has a few limitations, it addresses a significant problem with a well-designed and thoroughly evaluated approach. The ability to improve faithfulness while maintaining editability, along with the easy integration into current pipelines increases its potential impact. While not groundbreaking, it presents a significant improvement in a practical task.
